Maybe we can implement a simple iterative "fill the gaps" algorithm:
- Work with normalized max M=1. Initialize a=0? Or small.
- While progress, compute c=a*a. Deficit d_k = M - c_k ≥0. We want add a nonnegative increment h such that 2a*h + h*h ≤ d (elementwise). Since a nonnegative, sufficient to ensure 2a*h ≤ d (ignore h^2). This is a linear constraint: h≥0, (a*h)_k ≤ d_k/2. We want maximize sum h (or a weighted sum) to increase S. This is exactly a linear program similar to previous LP but with M fixed and using a*h ≤ deficit. Then update a += h (or a = a + θ h) and renormalize max to 1 (if max<1 scale up). This is a greedy LP/projection that adds mass where deficits are large. This may build a good sequence from scratch! It resembles water-filling and should produce a sequence with high total S under max≤1. Since constraints linear for a fixed a, we can solve the LP or approximate.

The LP: maximize ∑ h_i subject to h_i≥0 and for all k, ∑_i K_{k,i} h_i ≤ d_k/2, where K_{k,i}=a_{k-i}+a_{i-k} if in bounds (derivative coefficient) maybe. Matrix nonnegative. This is a packing LP. Pseudo:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    f=np.fft.rfft(a, 2*m) # or next pow
    return np.fft.irfft(f*f, 2*m)[:m]
```

Block perturbation algorithm:
```
def random_block_candidate(a, rng, mode):
    n=len(a); cand=a.copy()
    if mode==0: # scale contiguous block
       width = int(n * rng.uniform(0.01,0.25)); start=...
       factor = np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.2))
       cand[start:start+width] *= factor
    elif mode==1: # shift mass from low to high
       l = rng.integers(0,n//2); r = n - rng.integers(0,n//2)
       t = rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)
       # proportionally reduce prefix up to l by (1-t), increase suffix after r by (1+t) maybe
       cand[:l+1] *= (1-t)
       cand[r:] *= (1+t)
    elif mode==2: # add right tail
       eps = rng.uniform(0,0.5)
       tail = np.power(np.maximum(np.arange(n-1,-1,-1),1), -0.5)
       cand = cand + eps*tail
    elif mode==3: # add exponentially increasing ramp
       r = np.exp(rng.uniform(-1,1)*np.linspace(0,1,n))
       cand = (1-alpha)*cand + alpha*r
    cand = np.clip(cand,0,1000)
    cand = normalize(cand)
    return cand
```
We accept if score < current_score with maybe small tolerance; track best. This may find parameter improvements. But random search with 2400s on n=2048 can do many. We can run for max 1200s maybe. FFT gradient implementation for normalized S=1:
```
def peak_gradient(a, beta=20.0):
    c = fft_conv(a)
    M = max(c)
    if M <=0: return zeros
    # weights emphasize max; use normalized W to sum 1
    # To avoid overflow, w = exp(-beta*(M-c)/M) = exp(beta*(c/M -1))
    w = np.exp(np.clip(beta*(c/M - 1.0), -50.0, 0.0))
    s = w.sum()
    if s == 0: w center =1
    w /= s
    # h_i = 2 sum_j w_{i+j} a_j
    # compute via convolution a * w[::-1]
    N = 1 << (3*n-3).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    m=2*n-1
    idx = m-1 - np.arange(n)  # [m-1, m-2,...,m-n]
    h = 2*conv[idx]
    h = np.maximum(h, 0)
    # optionally subtract mean? Log update normalization handles.
    norm_h = h.sum()
    if norm_h >0: h /= norm_h
    return h
```
Check indices: For n=2, m=3, idx [2,1], conv length? N enough. Should work.
Update: `u = np.log(a + 1e-300); for step in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8]: a2=exp(u - step*h); normalize; score; choose best`. This is gradient descent. Step size maybe. Since h sums to1, step small. Run maybe 1000 iterations with line search. Could be effective.

However, function gradient of max (here w) times; objective scale invariant; if a normalized sum1, minimizing max c directly. We can minimize soft max by natural gradient; should reduce peaks. def param_family(n, rng=None):
   candidates=[]
   L=central_binomial_sequence(n)
   candidates.append(L/L.sum())
   # power family
   for p in [0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
       a = np.power(np.arange(n)+1, -p)
   # linear ramp on L
   for t in [-0.5,-0.2,0,0.2,0.5]:
       a = L * (1 + t*np.linspace(0,1,n-1))
   # left-right mix
   R = (np.arange(n-1,-1,-1)+1)**-0.5
   for t in [0,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5]:
       a = (1-t)*L + t*R
   # add right tail with small exponent p? a = L + eps*R (same)
   # random smooth shapes: a = L * exp(t*np.sin(2πx))? nonnegative.
```

Let's write helper functions:
```
import numpy as np, time, math

def _base_vec(n):
   a=np.empty(n)
   a[0]=1.0
   for i in range(n-1): a[i+1]=a[i]*(2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
   return a

def _normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s>0 and np.isfinite(s): a=a/s
   else: a=np.ones(1)
   return a

def _fft_conv(a):
   n=a.size; m=2*n-1
   N=1
   while N < m: N <<=1
   f=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(f*f,N)
   c=c[:m]
   # Clean small negative
   c[c<0] = 0.0 # But assignment in helper okay, no lambda
   return c
```
Need be careful `c[c<0]=0` modifies view; okay. For arrays of length maybe 1, fine.

`_fft_score(a)`:
```
c=_fft_conv(a); M=float(np.max(c)); S=float(np.sum(a)); if S<1e-120: return inf; return 2*a.size*M/(S*S)
```
Since normalized, S=1.

Gradient step candidate:

def _log_update(a, h, step):
    u=np.log(a + 1e-300)
    b=np.exp(u - step*h)
    return _normalize(b)
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start + max(0, float(budget_s)-60)
    N=int(8192)
    # maybe if previous height_sequence_1 length not huge, cap? no.
    best_a = _normalize(_base_vec(N))
    best_score = _fft_score(best_a)
    print(...)
    # parameter sweep
    x=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    L_raw=_base_vec(n)
    shapes=[]
    shapes.append(L_raw)
    # exponents
    for p in np.linspace(0.35,0.65,13): shapes.append(np.power(x+1, -p))
    # ramps
    for t in np.linspace(-0.5,0.75,16): shapes.append(L_raw*(1+t*(x/(n-1))))
    # left-right
    R_raw=np.power(np.maximum(n-1-x,0)+1, -0.5)
    for t in np.linspace(0,0.4,9): shapes.append((1-t)*L_raw + t*R_raw)
    for a in shapes:
       cand=_normalize(a)
       sc=_fft_score(cand)
       if finite < best: update
    # maybe evaluate direct best later
    if time < deadline:
       current_a=best_a.copy(); current_score=best_score
       no_improve=0
       iters=0
       beta=5.0
       while time.monotonic() < deadline and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 60:
          # every 200 iters increase beta, maybe reset no_improve
          grad=_gradient(current_a,beta)
          best_cand=current_a; best_sc=current_score
          for step in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.2):
             cand=_log_update(current_a, grad, step)
             sc=_fft_score(cand)
             if sc<best_sc: best_cand=cand; best_sc=sc
          if best_sc < current_score - 1e-15:
             current_a=best_cand; current_score=best_sc; no_improve=0
             if current_score < best_score:
                best_a=best_cand.copy(); best_score=current_score; print
          else:
             no_improve +=1
             # small random kick? use block random to escape
             cand = _random_block_cand(current_a, rng)
             sc = _fft_score(cand)
             if sc < current_score:
                current_a=cand; current_score=sc; no_improve=0
                if sc<best_score: update
          iters +=1
          if iters % 100 ==0: beta = min(50, beta*1.1)
          if no_improve > 800: break
    # If time remains, random local search from best for maybe up to 300s
    current_a=best_a.copy(); current_score=best_score
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
       cand = _random_block_cand(current_a, rng)
       sc = _fft_score(cand)
       if sc < current_score:
          current_a=cand; current_score=sc
          if sc < best_score:
             best_a=cand.copy(); best_score=sc; print
       # alternatively keep moving with annealing? We'll just hill climb.
    # Direct evaluate best and baseline
    final_seq = [float(v) for v in best_a]
    best_direct = safe_evaluate(final_seq)
    base_direct = safe_evaluate([float(v) for v in _normalize(_base_vec(N))])
    if not finite best_direct or best_direct > base_direct:
       return [float(v) for v in _normalize(_base_vec(N))]
    else:
       return final_seq
```

Random block candidate function:
```
def _random_block_cand(a, rng):
    n=a.size; c=a.copy()
    mode = int(rng.integers(0,5))
    if mode==0:
       width=int(n*rng.uniform(0.02,0.3)); if width<2: width=2; start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1)); factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.15))); c[start:start+width]*=factor
    elif mode==1:
       l=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n//2))); r=n-1-int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n//2)))
       if r <= l: return _normalize(c)
       t=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.4))
       c[:l]*=(1-t)
       c[r:]*=(1+t)
    elif mode==2:
       # add right singular
       tail=np.power(np.maximum(n-1-np.arange(n),0)+1, -0.5)
       eps=float(rng.uniform(0,0.3))
       c = c + eps*tail
    elif mode==3:
       # reverse right singular scaled sum
       tail=np.power(np.maximum(n-1-np.arange(n),0)+1, -0.5)
       tail = tail / tail.sum()
       mix=float(rng.uniform(0,0.2))
       c = (1-mix)*a + mix*tail
    else:
       # exponential ramp
       alpha=float(rng.uniform(0,0.3))
       ramp=np.exp(float(rng.uniform(-1,1))*np.linspace(0,1,n))
       ramp=ramp/ramp.sum()
       c=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*ramp
    return _normalize(c)
```
Need ensure no lambda. Good.

Gradient line search: If `step` too large, `np.exp` underflow to zeros; normalize handles. But if candidate sum zero? no.

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing

    F(a) = 2*n*max(a*a) / (sum(a))**2

where `a*a` is the full linear convolution.

The algorithm uses:
  * an exact "one-sided" baseline a_i = binom(2i, i)/4^i, whose
    convolution is exactly flat (=1) on the first half and decays after,
    giving F close to pi/2 for large n;
  * a deterministic parameter sweep over endpoint-singular / power / ramp
    families;
  * natural-gradient descent on the simplex using smoothed max-convolution
    peaks;
  * randomized hill climbing / block perturbations.

The function returns the best finite sequence found, with a reliable
fallback to the one-sided baseline.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


TARGET_TO_BEAT = 1.5027
FINAL_N = 4096


def _base_vec(n):
    """Exact left one-sided coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), truncated to n."""
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        a[i + 1] = a[i] * (2 * i + 1) / (2 * i + 2)
    return a


def _normalize(a):
    """Clip, remove nonfinite entries, and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    a[a > 1000.0] = 1000.0

    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)

    a *= (1.0 / s)
    return a


def _fft_convolve(a):
    """Linear convolution via real FFT, with tiny negative artifacts removed."""
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    N = 1
    while N < m:
        N <<= 1

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    out = out[:m]
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def _fft_score(a):
    """Fast approximate score using FFT convolution."""
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def _safe_evaluate(seq_list):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator if available, defensively."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq_list)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _peak_gradient(a, beta):
    """
    Natural-gradient direction for reducing the smoothed maximum convolution.

    a is normalized to sum 1. The returned h has nonnegative entries and
    sums to 1, suitable for log-domain updates:

        log a_i <- log a_i - step * h_i
    """
    n = a.size
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n

    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n

    # Emphasize peaks. The maximum has weight 1, below peaks are damped.
    x = c / M
    w = np.exp(np.clip(beta * (x - 1.0), -50.0, 0.0))
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w *= (1.0 / ws)

    m = 2 * n - 1

    # h_i = 2 * sum_j w_{i+j} a_j.
    # Compute via convolution with reversed w.
    N = 1
    need = 3 * n
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)

    idx = (m - 1) - np.arange(n)
    h = 2.0 * conv[idx]
    h[h < 0.0] = 0.0
    h[~np.isfinite(h)] = 0.0

    hs = float(np.sum(h))
    if not np.isfinite(hs) or hs <= 0.0:
        h = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    else:
        h *= (1.0 / hs)
    return h


def _log_step(a, h, step):
    """One natural-gradient step in log-space, normalized back to sum 1."""
    u = np.log(a + 1e-300)
    b = np.exp(u - step * h)
    return _normalize(b)


def _random_block_candidate(a, rng):
    """Random positive block/mixing perturbation."""
    n = a.size
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c = a.copy()
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 6))

    if mode == 0:
        # Scale a contiguous block.
        width = int(max(2, n * float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.35))))
        if width >= n:
            width = n
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.16)))
        c[start:start + width] *= factor

    elif mode == 1:
        # Transfer mass: damp a left block, boost a right block.
        l = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n // 2)))
        r = n - int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n // 2)))
        if r <= l:
            r = n - 1
            l = 0
        t = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))
        c[:l] *= (1.0 - t)
        c[r:] *= (1.0 + t)

    elif mode == 2:
        # Add a right-decaying singular tail.
        tail = np.power(np.maximum(n - 1 - x, 0.0) + 1.0, -0.5)
        eps = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.35))
        c = c + eps * tail

    elif mode == 3:
        # Mix in a normalized right singular tail.
        tail = np.power(np.maximum(n - 1 - x, 0.0) + 1.0, -0.5)
        tail = tail / max(1.0, float(np.sum(tail)))
        mix = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.25))
        c = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * tail

    elif mode == 4:
        # Mix in a smooth exponential ramp.
        shift = float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0))
        ramp = np.exp(shift * (x / max(1.0, float(n) - 1.0)))
        ramp = ramp / max(1.0, float(np.sum(ramp)))
        mix = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.35))
        c = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * ramp

    else:
        # Small coordinate reshuffling.
        for _ in range(3):
            i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            f = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12)))
            c[i] *= f
            c[j] /= f
            c[c < 0.0] = 0.0

    return _normalize(c)


def _candidate_family_scores(n):
    """
    Build and evaluate a deterministic family of structured candidates.

    Returns (best_array, best_score).
    """
    base = _base_vec(n)
    base_norm = _normalize(base)
    best_a = base_norm.copy()
    best_s = _fft_score(base_norm)

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    rn = max(1, n - 1)

    # Pure power-law shapes.
    for p in np.linspace(0.35, 0.70, 15):
        cand = np.power(x + 1.0, -p)
        cand = _normalize(cand)
        sc = _fft_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
            best_a = cand.copy()
            best_s = sc

    # Left one-sided with smooth ramps.
    for t in np.linspace(-0.4, 0.75, 18):
        cand = base * (1.0 + t * (x / rn))
        cand = _normalize(cand)
        sc = _fft_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
            best_a = cand.copy()
            best_s = sc

    # Left/right endpoint singular mixtures.
    right = base[::-1].copy()
    for alpha in (1.0, 0.95, 0.90, 0.85, 0.80):
        for beta in (0.0, 0.02, 0.04, 0.08, 0.12, 0.18, 0.25, 0.40):
            cand = alpha * base + beta * right
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            sc = _fft_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
                best_a = cand.copy()
                best_s = sc

    # Power-law mixtures left^p + beta*right^q.
    for p in (0.40, 0.50, 0.60):
        for q in (0.40, 0.50, 0.60):
            L = np.power(x + 1.0, -p)
            R = np.power(np.maximum(n - 1 - x, 0.0) + 1.0, -q)
            for beta in (0.0, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
                cand = L + beta * R
                cand = _normalize(cand)
                sc = _fft_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
                    best_a = cand.copy()
                    best_s = sc

    return best_a, best_s


def _run_gradient_phase(a, score, deadline):
    """Natural-gradient local search."""
    n = a.size
    current = a.copy()
    cur_score = score
    beta = 6.0
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    steps = (0.04, 0.08, 0.16, 0.32, 0.65, 1.10)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
        h = _peak_gradient(current, beta)

        best_cand = current
        best_sc = cur_score

        for step in steps:
            cand = _log_step(current, h, step)
            sc = _fft_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_cand = cand
                best_sc = sc

        accepted = False
        if best_sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
            current = best_cand
            cur_score = best_sc
            no_improve = 0
            accepted = True

        if not accepted:
            # Occasionally kick with random perturbation to escape.
            cand = _random_block_candidate(current, np.random.default_rng(np.random.SeedSequence([it, n, 7])))
            sc = _fft_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                current = cand
                cur_score = sc
                no_improve = 0
                accepted = True

        it += 1
        no_improve += 1 if not accepted else 0

        if it % 25 == 0:
            beta = min(45.0, beta * 1.03)

        if no_improve > 1200:
            break

    return current, cur_score


def _run_random_phase(a, score, deadline, rng):
    """First-improvement randomized hill climbing."""
    current = a.copy()
    cur_score = score
    checked = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
        cand = _random_block_candidate(current, rng)
        sc = _fft_score(cand)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
            current = cand
            cur_score = sc

        checked += 1
        if checked % 10000 == 0:
            # This phase is intentionally time-limited by the caller.
            pass

    return current, cur_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return the best nonnegative sequence found for the evaluator.

    Uses a moderate length (4096) so that direct O(n^2) convolution in the
    official evaluator remains quick, while still being large enough for the
    one-sided construction to approach its asymptotic constant.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 60.0)

    n = int(FINAL_N)

    # Baseline: exact one-sided sequence, normalized to sum 1.
    base_raw = _base_vec(n)
    base = _normalize(base_raw)
    base_s = _fft_score(base)

    best_a = base.copy()
    best_score = base_s

    print("[init] n=%d baseline_score=%.10f" % (n, best_score), flush=True)

    # If initial target is already unattainably low and our bound is known,
    # still run a bounded search; it closes quickly when no improvement exists.

    # Phase 1: deterministic structured family.
    fam_a, fam_s = _candidate_family_scores(n)
    if np.isfinite(fam_s) and fam_s < best_score:
        best_a = fam_a
        best_score = fam_s
        print("[param] score=%.10f" % best_score, flush=True)

    if best_score < TARGET_TO_BEAT:
        print("[stop] already below target %.6f" % TARGET_TO_BEAT, flush=True)

    # Phase 2: gradient local search if we have time.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 240.0:
        grad_a, grad_s = _run_gradient_phase(best_a, best_score, deadline - 360.0)
        if np.isfinite(grad_s) and grad_s < best_score:
            best_a = grad_a
            best_score = grad_s
            print("[grad] score=%.10f" % best_score, flush=True)

    # Phase 3: randomized hill climbing with remaining time.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 40.0:
        rand_a, rand_s = _run_random_phase(best_a, best_score, deadline - 10.0, rng)
        if np.isfinite(rand_s) and rand_s < best_score:
            best_a = rand_a
            best_score = rand_s
            print("[random] score=%.10f" % best_score, flush=True)

    # Authoritative verification.
    best_list = [float(v) for v in best_a]

    # Analytic baseline score for the exact one-sided raw vector.
    # For this vector, max(raw_conv) = 1 exactly, so score = 2n / (sum^2).
    s_raw = float(np.sum(base_raw))
    base_formula = float((2.0 * n) / (s_raw * s_raw)) if s_raw > 0.0 else float("inf")

    best_direct = _safe_evaluate(best_list)
    print("[verify] best_direct=%.10f base_formula=%.10f" % (best_direct, base_formula), flush=True)

    if np.isfinite(best_direct) and best_direct <= base_formula + 1e-9:
        return best_list

    # Robust fallback: one-sided baseline list.
    fallback = [float(v) for v in base]
    return fallback
```
